CORE-001: a failed backup establishment used to be swallowed and the pack carried on, so create_zip replaced the canonical archive with nothing to roll back to and a later verify failure deleted the replacement -- the last good archive simply gone. Securing the predecessor is now a PRECONDITION, retried 4x for a transient holder, and a refusal returns with the archive untouched. CORE-002: two stale-whole-config writers. The profile click mutated this window's snapshot and saved the WHOLE object unlocked, so a Bridge registration in between was rolled back by choosing a profile; routed through scoped_config_write and the refused save now restores the button instead of announcing a profile the next START will not use. The settings merge fell back to that same snapshot when load_config failed -- the one moment it must not guess -- and now fails closed. CORE-003: the retry contract settled as PORTABLE rather than by narrowing three tests the Ubuntu matrix could never pass; a missing file is still never retried. Diagnostics basename cut on both separators, because Path().name on POSIX read a whole Windows path as one filename and leaked the operator's directory into a record documented as redacted. CORE-004: both README release badges still advertised 0.2.2 against 0.2.3, pinned by a test

W2-001: the lane was closed INSIDE the finalization block, before campaign.json and the run state were committed, so a failed index write rolled the handoff off disk and left the dispatch COMPLETE pointing at a file that no longer existed with its worker freed; both the lane close and the project mirror moved past the commit, next to the proof-checked complete_for_run. Rollback errors were discarded at every one of the six call sites, so an artifact the rollback could not unlink was reported as a clean retriable failure -- now rollback_incomplete, non-retriable, naming the residual paths, same convention as ingest._ingest_failure. The regression then caught a third defect nobody had filed: the snapshot list was an if-quick3-else-SUPER_AUDIT branch written before the compress profile existed, so a CM campaign's canonical handoff was never snapshotted and could not be rolled back at all; derived from canonical_artifact_kind now, the same switch the writer uses. W2-003: project_id and project_name went into ONE set compared against a set holding each job's id AND name, so a project whose NAME equals another project's ID terminalized that other project's live audit, inherited its handoff as proof and had drift written into its lineage -- measured 2 closed for one project on the pre-fix code

- 03.09.26 23:04 [E-710] [parent: E-709] [T-139] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-948568b79a484ecdb75c6868bb22f408] RUN: BUILD -- wave 2 finished: W2-002, W2-004, W2-005. W2-002, the INAUDIT pair: _move_to_status published the new status into the SOURCE metadata and then moved body and metadata as two independent replaces, so a sharing violation on the second left the body in archive and the metadata in inbox and get() answered ARCHIVED with empty text -- permanently, because recovery only ever scanned the inbox. Copy-then-delete now, so the destination pair is complete before the source stops existing; recovery walks all three lifecycle directories and reunites by capture id, keeping the newer metadata when a crash left two intact copies. And recovery detected an undecodable body, then re-decoded it one line later with no handler: the constructor raised UnicodeDecodeError, the next startup found an empty inbox, and the preserved bytes were unreachable through the API for good -- the digest comes from raw bytes now and get() reports body_undecodable instead of raising. W2-004: a failed jobs.json write left memory holding a state no restart could reproduce (CANCELLED in the process, QUEUED on disk); memory is restored from the last durable document. A failed generation write reported a COMMITTED transition as a failure and the retry then said invalid_transition -- it is a notification, deferred and repaired on the next write, and cancelling an already-CANCELLED dispatch is idempotent. W2-005: the guard read absence of a window as proof the mutex owner was dead, and a process between CreateMutexW and window.show() looks exactly like that, so a rapid second launch opened a second GUI -- two config writers, the amplifier for CORE-002. Liveness is asked about the PROCESS now via a recorded owner pid; on POSIX a held lock no longer falls through app.main's leftover-lock branch, which could never activate anything and therefore failed open by construction

(1) refresh_runs called audits.refresh_project for every project with a retained job, which is force_rescan -- the periodic dashboard INVALIDATED the AuditIndexer's working directory-signature cache on every 4s tick and re-read every wave file of every project with a job, changed or not; it uses get_snapshot now, so the signature cache decides, and generation/watcher events still invalidate the exact project that changed. (2) The GUI fetched /v1/browser/status twice per repaint -- refresh_runs once, runtime_status once -- so refresh_runs accepts an already-fetched snapshot and the composite refresh hands it the one it has. (3) Every matched intent called intents.update(), each one reacquiring the cross-process lock and reparsing the whole journal just to learn the values were already correct (101 reads / ~3MB JSON per 100-run pass); AuditStartIntentStore.reconcile batches them under one lock, one parse, one write only if something moved

- 04.09.26 00:52 [E-714] [parent: E-713] [T-139] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-f56f6d843b5746fc983d327877c81d37] RUN: BUILD -- PERF-001 and PERF-003 of audit/1.md, the last two on the receipt, both userscript. PERF-001: the bounded 200-record INAUDIT spool had unbounded per-cycle cost. putInauditSpool re-stringified EVERY other record to recompute the aggregate size on every retry-metadata write (O(N^2) local work: 20 records = 36ms, 100 = 844ms, 200 = 3258ms with an instant failure), so each record now carries its serialized size_bytes and admission adds stored numbers. And a Bridge outage -- the exact condition the spool exists for -- issued one 12s-timeout request PER due record while holding inauditCaptureFlushInFlight, so a full queue could sit 200x12s = 40 minutes inside one flush with newer captures unable to progress; the endpoint is probed once now and, if it is down, the remaining due records get their retry window without being charged an attempt for a send that never happened. PERF-003: the MutationObserver knew which subtree changed and the locality was thrown away -- every burst re-ran attachInauditActions(document), revisiting every historical turn with a stable-check, an action lookup, a querySelectorAll('pre') and a per-block scan. Measured with controls already attached so every call added nothing: 20 turns = 162 selector calls, 100 = 802, 300 = 2402 / 116.9ms, growing with conversation length on the main thread. Dirty turns are derived from record.target and addedNodes and only those are scanned; a mutation resolving to no turn still falls back to the full scan, and there is no permanent processed flag, so a React-replaced action bar in an OLD turn is repaired. Widget 0.0.47

- 04.09.26 01:58 [E-727] [parent: E-726] [T-141] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-530cbb3714714711be8228bcd4f5e4ce] RUN: BUILD -- audit/2.md wave 1 and 2 plus PERF-002/PERF-004. CORE-001: @updateURL was hardcoded to 17843 while BridgeConfig.port is operator-editable, so moving the port silently killed auto-update forever (Tampermonkey persists the endpoint from the metadata) -- the served script now rewrites both directives from the endpoint it is answering on, loopback-only, and there is one pair instead of two to drift. CORE-004: the unittest.main() guard sat in the MIDDLE of test_components.py, so direct discovery ran 10 of 25 tests; moved to the end, with an AST-parsed test that a class can never again be defined after it. CORE-005: _worker_profile_is_live answered active_workers>0, which is lane registration, not the profile -- false positive on a foreign widget tab and, worse, a guaranteed false negative on a fresh profile where the userscript is not installed yet, so the installer waited the full 25s for an impossible condition and then opened a second window anyway; it now enumerates windows by --user-data-dir, with the Bridge answer kept as the off-Windows fallback. CORE-002/CORE-003/CORE-006 are already repaired in the audit/1.md layer and are recorded as such rather than re-fixed. W2-002: mirror_project_audits picked a free layer with next_layer_number, which only OBSERVES, then wrote with plain write_bytes -- two writers took the same number and one audit vanished while both reported success; publication is O_CREAT|O_EXCL with retry onto the next free number. W2-005: the persisted generation was read AFTER _load_jobs, so a restart doing its designed recovery republished at generation 1 and a UI that had seen 2 ignored it and everything after; loaded first, and advanced under the jobs lock so a second Bridge cannot regress it. W2-006: stop() only set the event, so shutdown proceeded to server_close and PID removal while a tick was still inside ensure_capacity -- it joins now, checks the event immediately before the one irreversible side effect, and clears the thread handle so start() works again. W2-007: the marker test patched LOCALAPPDATA while get_user_runtime_dir prefers AUDAPACK_RUNTIME_DIR, which conftest sets session-wide, so it wrote a revocation marker into the shared secrets dir and every later Bridge-auth test inherited it -- green depended on collection order. PERF-002: check_auth called load_config on EVERY authenticated request, which parses the config, migrates it and stats every project path (8.1ms at 300 projects) just to compare a bearer token; the canonical token file is read on (mtime_ns,size) change, so rotation still needs no restart. PERF-004: ensure_fresh_archive walked the tree with no exclusion matcher, stat-ing the node_modules/.venv/.git weight that packing omits (141ms at 20k excluded files) -- one shared eligible_source_files traversal now, short-circuiting on the first newer file, and a first pack does no freshness walk at all
- 04.09.26 02:12 [E-728] [parent: E-727] [T-141] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-d8b241ac20904986b45f0d1ecc971b92] RUN: BUILD -- W2-004 closed, the last open finding of audit/2.md. The recovering launcher CLOSED its handle to the existing primary mutex and kept only a separate _RECOVERY_MUTEX, so when the zombie finally exited the primary named object went with it: a third launcher saw no primary mutex at all, created a fresh one, never consulted the recovery mutex, and was admitted as a second full GUI -- two config writers, the same amplifier CORE-002 is about. The primary handle is held for the recovered instance's whole life (that is what keeps a named object alive), the recovery mutex only serializes competing recovery launchers, and release() closes each exactly once. - 04.09.26 10:16 [E-740] [parent: E-739] [T-142] [agent: opencode] [op: checkpoint-1dc537edf9e64cf29ace89915f97932d] RUN: BUILD -- audit/3.md, first half. CORE-003: MANDATORY_EXCLUDES ('must never be packaged': token.txt, *.token, *.secret) was enforced only by the directory walk -- the single-file branch checked cancellation and symlinks then wrote the file straight in, so --pack token.txt and the Explorer context menu produced success=True with the secret inside an archive built to be uploaded to an AI auditor. Refused loudly now, at create_zip so every entry point inherits it. CORE-004: cross-wave run-id equality is a pure function of the pasted text and ran AFTER resolve_or_register_project and mkdir, so an unknown project pasting mismatched ids was rejected -- saying 'all writes rolled back' -- having already created config.json, a durable registry row and audits/SIDE1/NEW_BAD; the check moved into prepare, registration is commit. CORE-005: every archive alias went into ONE unordered set and blank values passed through safe_archive_stem, which maps '' to the literal 'Archive' -- so a project with no archive_name matched a stray generic Archive.zip, and a NEWER display-name archive beat the configured archive_name; ordered phases now, blank contributes no alias. CORE-006: diagnostics promised 'never tokens or content' and copied worker-supplied error/recovery verbatim -- Bearer, token=, and both path grammars survived into the JSON the UI offers to copy; redaction now runs before truncation so a limit cannot cut a marker and leave the secret. W2-003: a failed claim_job persist rolled back the job but not the worker, leaving the lane RESERVED for a claim that never committed; worker ownership is snapshotted with the jobs, and last_seen_at deliberately is not -- a heartbeat is a fact about the window. W2-004: STARTED/AUDITING -> COMPLETE required NO proof, so an empty ACK terminalized a run, freed the lane and let a second audit for the same project in; proof is now a handoff that exists and hashes right, or the Bridge's own campaign probe, and an unproven ACK holds at FINALIZING -- which is what it actually means. Also corrected my own PERF-002 from the previous layer: I had keyed the token on (mtime_ns,size), and a same-length rotation inside one timestamp tick was invisible; a stale credential is the one thing an auth path must not serve, and the 40-byte read was never the cost

The irreversible effect came before the record: ensure_capacity spawned the browser and only then wrote the LAUNCHING row, so a failed ledger write lost all knowledge of an already-open window and the retry opened the same slot again -- measured launches [(1,1)] with no journal, then [(1,1),(1,1)] once persistence returned. A RESERVED row is now durable before the spawn and counts as occupied, with the boot grace expiring it if the launch never happened; launch_slot got the same ordering. And an unreadable ledger became a fresh {generation:1, slots:{}}, which is not an empty pool but an UNKNOWN one: every slot read vacant so the next pass opened windows onto slots that already had one, and generation 1 collided with the install's first era. Corruption is quarantined and the ledger rebuilt from the workers that demonstrably exist -- a registered worker proves its slot AND its generation, so the era never goes backwards. 909 pytest, 265 widget, ruff clean

Publication is now staged-fsync-replace under a cross-process lock, deletion requires every SUPPLIED expected field present and equal (fail closed on unreadable), and both share the one lock closing the read/replace/unlink TOCTOU with a starting successor. The second half: stop_bridge read bridge.pid AFTER the endpoint went offline, so a successor starting in that gap had its record deleted by the retiring controller; the target identity is now captured from a LIVE /health BEFORE the shutdown request -- /health gained a pid field beside instance_nonce for exactly that -- and cleanup compares against that captured identity. W2-005: os.kill(pid, 0) raises OverflowError beyond the platform's signed-int range, measured 4000000000 on POSIX, and nothing caught it, so a corrupt owner record could crash the launcher's second-instance handling; the probe is now total over every parsed integer, garbage parses fail closed to alive, and huge values read as not-ours. Nine new regressions, red pre-fix where the module allows. 935 pytest, 265 widget, ruff clean

ROOT CAUSE B proven: with no cached mapping the event callback's fallback window_pid(hwnd) answers conhost.exe, not the durable PowerShell launch PID, so the registration could never be mapped and the guardian went blind indefinitely. New title-integrity heartbeat (1000 ms) retires dead launches, re-resolves/repairs bindings and restores drift; it starts only while a managed instance exists and is fully stopped with zero instances (no 250 ms poller). New bind_hwnd() lets InstanceMonitor's PROVEN launch_pid+hwnd+correlation_token association be fed in (conhost native PID, never the corrupted title). Ownership identity now carries launch pid + creation token + generation + exact hwnd + native window pid + correlation token, so a reused HWND can never be renamed. P1: new persisted UI settings project_double_click_action / project_double_click_launcher_id with stable values and deterministic migration; populated-project-row double-click now dispatches to the configured canonical handler and the launcher action reuses _on_open_with_launcher (focus existing, never duplicate); group/empty-slot behaviour unchanged; Settings gained Project row double-click + Double-click launcher controls.
